title: Native three-bit attention predictor mechanism probe
tool: Native Engine diagnostic and existing Python supervision
---
One prospectively frozen mechanism pair runs the current three-bit standalone weights twice at a real 14-GB ceiling, first with the retained boundary forecast, then with the existing plain attention tap and its one-layer window. Both processes pass physical ceilings and the existing pilot eligibility policy. All three declared workloads have exact token and text equality. This is a single directional probe with uncontrolled filesystem caching, not a repeated speed qualification or grounds for changing the product default.

The Engine change admits the attention tap only through an explicit native standalone diagnostic with a complete lookahead reserve and no ambient overrides. The original and reference configurations remain unchanged. The recipe verifier now requires the complete retained uncorrected scheduler configuration, so a different experimental forecast cannot relabel itself as the compiled startup recipe. The diagnostic checks the actual loaded scheduler. Twenty-two Python runner tests and ten native protocol/lookahead/pack groups pass. Both plan-only proposals have identical pool capacity and budgets. Release build provenance is preserved; source is committed as b771387a5dd87f5b43b7ee52d609d0a6229e3f82.

The probe reuses the existing supervision and exclusion functions. A parent-bound caffeinate assertion prevents idle sleep/activity expiry for at most thirty minutes and ends with the probe. No Photos process is paused in this pair. One Spotlight CPU burst resets the first admission interval before any model loads. All allocations and workloads remain unchanged. Startup timing is not the mechanism claim: the diagnostic attention path calls the same internal Engine initializer directly, while the boundary arm retains the maintained-pack factory and its repeated metadata validation.

Exact input, implementation, output and supervision bytes follow, each compressed with zlib and base64 and bound by original byte count and SHA-256. The full build source archive and binary are identified by the reconstructible build receipt; no model payload is embedded.
